### [1] [Robust Cross-Domain Generalization Using Unlabeled Target Data with Source-Domain Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29122)
*Yuyue Zhou,Shrimanti Ghosh,Michael,Xie,Justin JY Kim,Jessica Knight,Steel McDonald,Vincent Man,Jacob L. Jaremko,Abhilash Hareendranathan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A label-efficient ultrasound domain adaptation method uses target-domain self-supervised pretraining plus a confidence-aware ensemble to improve pediatric wrist fracture segmentation across devices, achieving over 6% Dice improvement without target labels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical ultrasound AI models trained with dense annotations often fail on images from different scanners or sites because of domain shift. Collecting new dense labels for each device is expensive, labor-intensive, and constrained by privacy, so the paper aims to improve cross-device robustness without target-domain annotations.

Method: The method uses labeled source-domain supervised training together with unlabeled target-domain self-supervised pretraining. It combines masked image modeling and contrastive learning to learn structural representations from the target domain, then uses a confidence-aware infusion head to adaptively integrate ensemble predictions.

Result: On 318 images from 62 pediatric POCUS videos, using source data from a Philips Lumify probe and unlabeled target data from a TeleMED probe, the proposed approach significantly improved target-domain performance by more than 6% Dice over the baseline.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that target-informed self-supervised pretraining combined with a confidence-aware ensemble can substantially improve cross-device generalization for pediatric wrist fracture ultrasound segmentation without requiring target-domain labels, yielding more than a 6% Dice gain on the target device while preserving privacy.

Abstract: It is often desirable to generalize medical imaging AI models trained with dense annotations to data acquired from different ultrasound scanners or clinical sites; however, retraining these models with new annotations is often difficult and costly. We examine this challenge in pediatric wrist fracture assessment using point-of-care ultrasound (POCUS), where fractures are common and can be effectively triaged via ultrasound. AI has shown radiologist-level performance for fracture detection, often aided by high-quality bony structure segmentation. However, due to significant domain shifts, models perform poorly on data from other centers or probes, and obtaining segmentation labels across devices is impractical due to manual annotation effort and data privacy concerns. To address this, we propose a target-informed self-supervised pretraining and model-ensemble strategy. Specifically, our approach combines masked image modeling (MIM) and contrastive learning to learn target-domain structural representations without labels, and introduces a confidence-aware infusion head to adaptively integrate predictions. The source dataset, collected with a Philips Lumify probe, contained dense labels, while the target dataset, acquired with a TeleMED portable probe, was unlabeled. The datasets were kept strictly separate throughout the entire process. Our method used labeled source data for supervised training and leveraged target-domain pretraining to improve generalization. On 318 images from 62 pediatric POCUS videos, this approach significantly improved cross-device performance, achieving over 6% Dice improvement on the target domain versus the baseline. These results demonstrate a label-efficient and privacy-preserving approach for cross-device-robust ultrasound AI, offering a framework that can be extended to multi-center studies or federated learning setups.

</details>


### [2] [SalsaAgent: A multimodal embodied language model for interactive dance generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29219)
*Payam Jome Yazdian,Zoe Stanley,Angelica Lim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SalsaAgent uses an LLM plus motion/audio/relation tokens and a diffusion decoder to generate responsive full-body salsa dancing, outperforming baselines on quality and coordination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to enable socially aware robots and interactive virtual agents to participate in human-like bidirectional, nonverbal interaction, especially coordinated and reactive partner dancing, where motion must stay synchronized with both a human leader and background music.

Method: The method introduces SalsaAgent, which extends a large language model with discrete motion tokens, pairwise relation tokens, and audio inputs. It adds new full-body and motion-relation token vocabularies, fine-tunes the LLM using automatically generated text descriptions of skeleton dynamics for grounding, and uses a two-stage token-to-diffusion pipeline to synthesize final dance motion.

Result: Both subjective and objective evaluations show significant improvements over baselines in generated motion quality, coordination with music, responsiveness to the dance partner, and consistency of two-person spatial behavior.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that SalsaAgent effectively generates expressive full-body salsa dance responses for a humanoid/virtual follower by modeling interaction as multimodal token passing within an LLM framework, achieving better motion quality, music alignment, partner coordination, and stable two-person spatial behavior than prior baselines.

Abstract: Interaction between humanoids involves bidirectional and nonverbal reactivity, coordination and synchrony. Toward socially aware robots and interactive virtual agents, we present SalsaAgent, a language model that generates expressive, full-body salsa dance motions in reaction to a human leader and against a contextual music backdrop. We formulate interaction as nonverbal motion token passing, extending the vocabulary of a large language model (LLM) to process discrete motion tokens, pairwise relation tokens, and audio. Our contributions include new tokens for full-body and motion relations, LLM fine-tuning using automatically derived text descriptions of skeleton dynamics for token grounding, and a two-stage token-to-diffusion pipeline. Subjective and objective evaluations demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach in terms of motion quality, music and partner coordination, and consistent two-person spatial behavior, with significant improvements over baselines.

</details>


### [3] [Turbulence-Robust Dynamic Object Segmentation with Multi-Signal Priors and SAM2 Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29292)
*Bolian Peng,Ying Tang,Xu Liu,Long Sun,Xiaoqiang Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free pipeline combining motion, semantic priors, background modeling, proposal fusion, and SAM2 refinement was used for turbulent-scene dynamic object segmentation, reaching 0.4250 mIoU and 0.4572 mDice without any task-specific training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address Dynamic Object Segmentation in Turbulence, where atmospheric turbulence causes pseudo-motion, blur, and intermittent visibility, making any single cue, especially motion alone, unreliable. A training-free multi-signal approach is adopted to improve robustness without task-specific training.

Method: The method is a training-free segmentation pipeline that fuses multiple pretrained and non-trained signals: RAFT for dense motion estimation, DINOv2 for semantic objectness priors, ViBe for background anomaly modeling, manually calibrated proposal fusion for combining cues, and SAM2 for box-prompted mask refinement.

Result: On the official CVPR 2026 UG2+ Challenge Track 3 leaderboard, the submitted method achieves 0.425041 mIoU and 0.457206 mDice.

Conclusion: The report concludes that a fully training-free, inference-only multi-signal segmentation pipeline is viable for dynamic object segmentation under severe atmospheric turbulence, achieving competitive benchmark performance, while indicating that stronger temporal association and task-specific adaptation could further improve results.

Abstract: This technical report presents our solution for the CVPR 2026 UG2+ Challenge Track 3: Dynamic Object Segmentation in Turbulence (DOST). We design a training-free multi-signal segmentation pipeline that combines pretrained motion estimation, self-supervised semantic priors, background anomaly modeling, manually calibrated proposal fusion, and SAM2-based mask refinement. The method uses RAFT for dense motion responses, DINOv2 for semantic objectness priors, ViBe for training-free background modeling, and pretrained SAM2 for box-prompt mask refinement.
  Instead of optimizing an end-to-end segmentation network, our system operates entirely in inference mode. This design is suitable for the DOST setting, where severe atmospheric turbulence produces pseudo-motion, blur, and intermittent target visibility, making a single motion cue unreliable. The final submitted masks are evaluated by the official leaderboard, which reports 0.425041 mIoU and 0.457206 mDice. Since no task-specific model training or fine-tuning is performed, stronger learned temporal association, adaptive proposal selection, or task-specific adaptation may further improve the system.

</details>


### [4] [Pocket-Dentist: On-Device Dental Image Understanding via Efficient Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29299)
*Kai Bian,Xucheng Guo,Bin Chen,Lingyan Ruan,Yiran Shen,Ting Dang,Hong Jia*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 论文提出了一个面向效率的牙科多模态问答基准，并证明小模型在牙科任务上可能比大模型更准、更快、更省内存，适合手机端本地部署。 / The paper presents an efficiency-focused benchmark for dental multimodal QA and shows that smaller models can be more accurate, faster, and more memory-efficient than larger ones, enabling practical on-device deployment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有牙科视觉语言模型评测在数据集、任务定义和指标上较为割裂，并且通常忽略计算成本；这阻碍了模型在非专科中心开展及时、低硬件依赖、保护隐私的牙科筛查应用。 / Existing evaluations of dental VLMs are fragmented across datasets, task definitions, and metrics, and usually ignore computational cost. This limits real-world use for timely, low-resource, privacy-preserving dental screening outside specialist centers.

Method: 作者提出 Pocket-Dentist，一个关注效率的牙科多模态问答基准，整合了3个数据集、约1,159名患者、5类任务和7项评测指标，并在14个典型VLM上进行系统评测；同时将微调后的2B模型部署到 iPhone 17 Pro 上验证本地推理可行性。 / The authors introduce Pocket-Dentist, an efficiency-aware benchmark for dental multimodal question answering, combining 3 datasets, about 1,159 patients, 5 task types, and 7 metrics. They systematically evaluate 14 representative VLMs and deploy a finetuned 2B model on an iPhone 17 Pro to validate on-device inference.

Result: 实验发现，约2B参数的紧凑型VLM在牙科图像理解上优于更大模型，同时显著降低计算成本。微调后的 Pocket-Dentist-2B 在 iPhone 17 Pro 上每个样本处理时间为4.31秒，相比7B基线延迟降低4.9倍、内存占用降低2.3倍。 / Results show that compact VLMs around 2B parameters outperform larger models on dental image understanding while using substantially fewer resources. The finetuned Pocket-Dentist-2B runs locally on an iPhone 17 Pro at 4.31 s per sample, with 4.9x lower latency and 2.3x lower memory use than a 7B baseline.

Conclusion: 在牙科多模态问答中，紧凑型视觉语言模型不仅更高效，而且在该基准下往往比更大模型更准确，更适合本地化、隐私保护的临床预筛查部署。 / In dental multimodal QA, compact vision-language models are not only more efficient but can also be more accurate than larger models, making them better suited for local, privacy-preserving clinical prescreening.

Abstract: Evaluations of dental vision-language models remain fragmented across datasets, task definitions and metrics, and often ignore their computational cost. This limits their widespread deployment for dental screening outside specialist centres, where timely inference, limited hardware, and local handling of patient images are vital for practical, privacy-preserving clinical prescreening. Here we present Pocket-Dentist, an efficiency-aware benchmark for dental multimodal question answering that brings together three datasets spanning approximately 1,159 patients, five task types and seven metrics. Across typical 14 VLMs, our results reveals an interesting observation: compact VLMs (e.g., 2B-parameter models) outperform larger VLMs in accuracy while requiring substantially lower computational costs in dental image understanding. Deployed locally on an iPhone 17 Pro, our finetuned compact VLM Pocket-Dentist-2B processed each sample in 4.31 s, reducing latency by 4.9-fold and memory use by 2.3-fold compared with a 7B baseline.

</details>


### [5] [ViASNet: A Video Ad Saliency Network for Predicting Dynamic Saliency and Viewer Engagement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29302)
*Jianping Ye,Michel Wedel*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper builds a 3D U-Net-based saliency model for short video ads that uses video, audio, and scene meaning to predict viewer attention; it works well on eye-tracking data and can help diagnose and improve ad effectiveness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Short-form video advertising is increasingly important across TV, social media, and e-commerce, creating a need for accurate automated prediction of where and when viewers look. Such prediction can help explain ad effectiveness and optimize ad content design.

Method: The paper proposes ViASNet, a deep dynamic saliency prediction model based on 3D U-Net. It integrates audio influence and scene semantics, is evaluated on 151 video advertisements with eye-tracking data from about 20 viewers per ad, and is further analyzed with ablation studies and frame-wise saliency entropy diagnostics.

Result: The model performs well on the collected video-ad eye-tracking dataset. Ablation experiments identify important factors affecting performance, and frame-by-frame entropy of predicted saliency maps helps detect ads or scenes with weak viewer engagement, demonstrated on 15 unseen ads.

Conclusion: ViASNet can effectively predict dynamic visual attention in short-form video ads by combining spatiotemporal, audio, and semantic scene information, and can support faster ad design evaluation through automated saliency analysis.

Abstract: The digital media landscape has seen a pervasive shift toward short-form video advertising on TV, social media and e-commerce platforms. The present study focuses on deep saliency prediction for short-form video advertising. Deep saliency models have been used to generate predictions of human eye fixation patterns with the purpose of enhancing user interaction with digital technology and optimizing its design. For video ads, dynamic saliency maps capture where and when viewers are looking, revealing why video ads are effective, and how their content should be optimized. We develop and test a new deep dynamic saliency prediction model called ViASNet (Video Ad Saliency Network), which has an architecture founded on the 3D U-Net, and accommodates the influence of audio and the semantic meaning of scenes. We assess the model's performance on 151 video ads, each seen by about 20 viewers wile their eye movements were tracked, and explore the critical factors influencing model performance through ablation experiments. We calculate the entropy of the predicted saliency maps frame-by-frame as a diagnostic tool to identify ads and scenes that fail to engage viewers, and illustrate its use on test data of 15 unseen ads. Our study reveals that ad design and testing can be sped up considerably through automated systems built on deep saliency models such as ViASNet.

</details>


### [6] [Rethinking FID Through the Geometry of the Reference Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29335)
*Yunghee Lee,Byeonghyun Pak*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FID can misjudge improving image quality because its behavior depends on reference dataset geometry; denser, lower-rank datasets favor better FID trends, so benchmarking should account for dataset geometry rather than relying on FID alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although FID is the standard metric for evaluating image generators, practitioners often observe cases where lower FID does not align with visibly better samples. The paper aims to explain when and why this mismatch occurs.

Method: The paper conducts a controlled study over six datasets, analyzing how FID changes as sample quality improves, and relates these changes to dataset geometry using measures such as distributional density and effective rank. It further performs precision/recall attribution and ablations over alternative feature spaces and distance metrics.

Result: Across six datasets, distributional density and effective rank significantly explain FID behavior: concentrated datasets tend to show more favorable FID improvements, while dispersed datasets can show worse FID even when sample quality improves. Precision/recall analysis and ablations support the robustness of this finding.

Conclusion: FID trends are partly governed by the geometry of the reference dataset; therefore, FID alone is not a universally reliable proxy for perceptual sample quality and should be interpreted together with dataset geometry.

Abstract: Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) is widely used to evaluate image generators, yet lower FID does not always correspond to better sample quality. We show that this mismatch depends in part on the geometry of the reference dataset. In a controlled study across six datasets, distributional density and effective rank significantly explain how FID changes as sample quality improves. Concentrated datasets tend to yield more favorable FID trends, whereas more dispersed datasets can make FID worsen despite better samples. Attribution to precision and recall and ablations with alternative feature spaces and distances support the same conclusion. These results suggest that distributional metrics should be interpreted together with the geometry of the reference dataset for more reliable benchmarking.

</details>


### [7] [Orthogonal Negative Guidance in Attention Feature Space for Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29390)
*Jungmin Ko,Jungwon Park,Jimyeong Kim,Changin Choi,Wonseok Lee,Wonjong Rhee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free attention-space guidance method removes unwanted concepts by subtracting only the part of negative features that is orthogonal to positive features, improving suppression accuracy while keeping image quality high.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current concept suppression methods in text-to-image generation, such as prompt negation, post-hoc editing, and negative guidance, often fail to explicitly remove target objects or attributes and can degrade image quality. The work aims to achieve explicit absence control without harming overall generation quality.

Method: The paper proposes Orthogonal Negative Guidance for MM-DiT-based text-to-image transformers. It operates in the attention output space by orthogonalizing negative-prompt attention features with respect to positive-prompt features, then subtracting only the orthogonal component to remove undesired concepts while retaining desired semantics.

Result: On FLUX-dev and FLUX-schnell, the method achieves a strong trade-off among concept suppression, prompt alignment, and image quality. In human evaluation, it outperforms the second-best baseline by 18.78%, and it supports both multi-concept suppression and adjustable suppression strength.

Conclusion: A training-free orthogonal negative guidance method in attention feature space can suppress unwanted concepts more effectively than existing approaches while preserving prompt alignment and image quality, and it also supports multi-concept and controllable suppression.

Abstract: Text-to-image (T2I) models have become increasingly capable of generating high-quality images. Yet, enforcing the explicit absence of a specified object or attribute remains a fundamentally challenging problem. Existing approaches, including prompt negation, post-hoc editing, and negative guidance, remain insufficient for explicit concept suppression, often failing to remove the target concept or degrading overall image quality. To this end, we propose Orthogonal Negative Guidance in attention feature space, a training-free method that operates in the attention output space of MM-DiT-based T2I transformers. Our method orthogonalizes negative-prompt attention features with respect to positive-prompt features and subtracts only the orthogonal component, suppressing unwanted concepts while preserving desired semantics. Experiments on FLUX-dev and FLUX-schnell show that our method achieves favorable trade-offs between concept suppression, prompt alignment, and image quality. In human evaluation, our method outperforms the second-best baseline by 18.78%. We further show that our method supports multi-concept suppression and adjustable concept suppression.

</details>


### [8] [RadioFormer3D: Weakly Supervised 3D Radio Map Estimation in Low-Altitude Airspace via Generative Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29538)
*Zheng Fang,Junjie Liu,Kangjun Liu,Jianguo Zhang,Yaowei Wang,Ke Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A 3D weakly supervised radio map model, RadioFormer3D, uses a Fourier encoder, volumetric decoder, and a joint integrity loss to reconstruct sparse volumetric spectrum data more accurately, especially at unseen heights.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing radio map estimation methods are mainly 2D, but emerging wireless applications in 3D environments require radio maps across both horizontal and vertical dimensions. Moving to 3D is difficult because data become sparser in space and supervision across continuous altitudes is limited.

Method: The paper extends RadioFormer to 3D by adding a Fourier-based sampling encoder for sparse 3D measurements, a volumetric decoder for volumetric spectrum reconstruction, and a Joint Spectrum Integrity Loss that combines volume-level pseudo-label supervision, map-level geometry-aware radio rendering, and pixel-level localized constraints.

Result: Experiments on multiple radio map datasets show that RadioFormer3D outperforms representative baseline methods overall, especially in reconstructing radio maps at unlabeled altitudes, and offers a favorable balance between reconstruction quality and inference efficiency.

Conclusion: RadioFormer3D is an effective weakly supervised 3D radio map estimation model that reconstructs volumetric spectrum maps from sparse measurements and generalizes well to unlabeled altitudes, while preserving a strong accuracy-efficiency trade-off.

Abstract: With the emergence of wireless applications in three-dimensional environments, such as the low-altitude airspace and 3D heterogeneous networks, radio map estimation is increasingly required to characterize signal propagation across both horizontal and vertical dimensions. However, extending radio map estimation from 2D to 3D remains challenging due to increased spatial sparsity and limited supervision across continuous altitudes. In this paper, we propose \textbf{\textit{RadioFormer3D}}, a specialized model for volumetric spectrum reconstruction under weak supervision. Building on the dual-stream, multi-granularity fusion architecture of \textit{RadioFormer}, \textit{RadioFormer3D} introduces a Fourier-based sampling encoder and a volumetric decoder to efficiently process sparse measurements in 3D space. To alleviate the lack of vertical supervision, we propose the \textbf{\textit{Joint Spectrum Integrity Loss}}, which integrates volume-level pseudo-label supervision, map-level geometry-aware radio rendering, and pixel-level localized constraints within a unified optimization scheme. This design enables the model to capture complex vertical structural relationships more effectively under sparse supervision. Extensive experiments across several radio map datasets show that \textit{RadioFormer3D} achieves superior overall performance compared to representative existing methods. In particular, it demonstrates improved reconstruction quality at unlabeled altitudes while maintaining a favorable trade-off between accuracy and inference efficiency, positioning it as a highly promising solution for future 3D environment-aware wireless networks.

</details>


### [9] [GiPL: Generative augmented iterative Pseudo-Labeling for Cross-Domain Few-Shot Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29539)
*Jiacong Liu,Shu Luo,Yikai Qin,Yaze Zhao,Yongwei Jiang,Yixiong Zou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GiPL improves cross-domain few-shot object detection by iteratively expanding sparse support labels with pseudo-labels and adding synthetic domain-aligned training images, leading to strong gains over prior methods on RUOD, CARPK, and CarDD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing vision-language foundation models show strong zero-shot transfer for CD-FSOD, but fine-tuning suffers from weak support-set utilization because annotations are sparse and from severe overfitting because target-domain samples are extremely limited.

Method: The method has two branches: (1) an iterative pseudo-label self-training branch that performs zero-shot inference on support images, merges reliable pseudo-annotations with ground-truth labels, and iteratively refines the detector; (2) a generative data augmentation branch using large vision-language models to synthesize domain-aligned, multi-object annotated images for training.

Result: Extensive experiments on three challenging CD-FSOD datasets show significant and consistent gains over state-of-the-art methods across 1-shot, 5-shot, and 10-shot settings.

Conclusion: GiPL is an efficient two-branch framework for cross-domain few-shot object detection that better exploits sparse support annotations and reduces overfitting, consistently surpassing prior state-of-the-art methods on RUOD, CARPK, and CarDD under 1/5/10-shot settings.

Abstract: Vision-language foundation models have shown promising zero-shot generalization for Cross-Domain Few-Shot Object Detection (CD-FSOD). However, they face two critical challenges in fine-tuning: insufficient support set utilization due to sparse single-instance annotations, and severe overfitting under extremely limited target-domain samples. To address these issues, this paper proposes GiPL, an efficient two-branch training framework.In the first branch, we design an iterative pseudo-label self-training paradigm, which performs zero-shot inference on the support set to generate reliable pseudo-annotations, fuses them with ground-truth labels, and iteratively optimizes the model to fully exploit support set data. In the second branch, we introduce generative data augmentation pipeline using large vision-language models, which synthesizes domain-aligned, multi-object annotated images to enrich training samples and suppress overfitting. Extensive experiments on three challenging CD-FSOD datasets (RUOD, CARPK, CarDD) under 1/5/10-shot settings demonstrate that GiPL consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods with significant performance gains.Code is available at \href{https://github.com/z-yaz/CDiscover}{CDiscover}.

</details>


### [10] [Learning Representations from 3D Gaussian Splats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29549)
*Julia Farganus,Krzysztof Żurawicki,Arkadiusz Gaweł,Weronika Jakubowska,Halina Kwaśnicka*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 本文系统比较了点式和图式几何学习模型在3D Gaussian Splatting场景分类上的效果，发现架构类型和是否利用高斯特征都会显著影响最终表征与分类性能。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 3DGS主要用于新视角合成，但其在场景理解任务中的潜力尚未被充分研究，因此作者希望系统评估哪些几何感知架构更适合学习3D Gaussian Splat表示。

Method: 对多种几何深度学习架构进行比较评估，包括点式模型和图式模型；在传统点云数据集与专门的Gaussian Splatting数据集上进行基准测试；将场景编码为潜在表示，并通过端到端分类、线性探测和聚类分析评估表征效果。

Result: 实验表明，不同架构家族之间存在一致的性能差异，且Gaussian特有属性对学习到的表示质量具有明显影响。

Conclusion: 不同几何深度学习架构在基于3D Gaussian Splatting的场景分类中表现存在稳定差异；高斯特有属性会显著影响表征质量，因此架构选择与输入特征设计都很关键。

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is a recent approach for scene rendering. Although primarily designed for view synthesis, its potential for scene understanding tasks remains underexplored. In this work, we conduct a comparative evaluation of various geometric deep learning architectures for the classification of 3D scenes represented using Gaussian Splatting. We benchmark point-based and graph-based models across both traditional point cloud datasets and dedicated Gaussian Splatting datasets. Scenes are embedded into latent representations, which are evaluated through end-to-end classification, linear probing, and clustering analysis. Our study provides insight into the suitability of different geometry-aware architectures and input feature configurations for learning effective 3D Gaussian Splat representations. The results highlight consistent differences between architectural families and reveal the impact of Gaussian-specific attributes on the quality of representation.

</details>


### [11] [TAE: Target-aware enhancer for nighttime UAV tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29558)
*Yanyan Chen,Ruigang Fu,Yu Song,Ping Zhong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TAE uses target-aware, bounding-box-guided low-light enhancement to improve nighttime UAV tracking, and achieves strong gains on the new DarkSOT benchmark and UAVDark135.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Low-light nighttime image degradation is a major bottleneck for all-day UAV-based single object tracking. Existing enhancement methods often fail to distinguish target from background, causing amplified background noise or loss of target features.

Method: The paper proposes TAE, a target-aware low-light enhancement framework guided by weak supervisory signals from tracking bounding boxes. It performs region-aware enhancement focused on the target area and uses an adaptive RGB multi-curve fusion mechanism for refined region-specific adjustment.

Result: The authors introduce both the TAE framework and the DarkSOT benchmark containing 268 sequences across 9 target categories. Experiments on DarkSOT and UAVDark135 show that TAE substantially improves nighttime tracking performance.

Conclusion: TAE is an effective target-aware low-light enhancement framework for nighttime UAV single object tracking, significantly improving tracking performance and showing strong robustness and generalization on DarkSOT and UAVDark135.

Abstract: Severe image degradation under low-light nighttime conditions constitutes a core bottleneck preventing all-day applications for UAV-based single object tracking. Existing image enhancement methods often struggle to distinguish between target and background regions, which can easily lead to amplified background noise or compromise target features. To overcome this limitation, we propose TAE, a target-aware low-light enhancement framework tailored for nighttime object tracking. Guided explicitly by weak supervisory signals from tracking bounding boxes, the framework performs region-aware enhancement to ensure operations focus on the target area. It further adopts an adaptive RGB multi-curve fusion mechanism to achieve refined modeling and adaptive adjustment across different regions. To facilitate research in this domain, we also contribute DarkSOT, a new benchmark for nighttime UAV tracking, comprising 268 sequences across 9 target categories. Experimental results on the DarkSOT and UAVDark135 demonstrate that TAE significantly improves tracking performance in low-light nighttime scenarios, exhibiting strong robustness and generalization. The DarkSOT dataset is available at https://github.com/Fu0511/DarkSOT-Dataset.

</details>


### [12] [DefSynUS: Real-time Patient-specific Intrahepatic Vessel Identification via Deformation-Aware CT-US Domain Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29570)
*Karl-Philippe Beaudet,Yordanka Velikova,Sidaty El Hadramy,Nassir Navab,Philippe Cattin,Juan Verde,Stéphane Cotin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A CT-driven, deformation-aware synthetic ultrasound framework identifies liver vessel branches in real time during laparoscopic surgery without preoperative ultrasound, showing promising phantom and early clinical feasibility results, but it still lacks multi-patient validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although laparoscopic ultrasound improves liver surgery safety by visualizing intrahepatic vessels in real time, accurate vessel identification is still difficult because of probe constraints, complex vascular anatomy, and tissue deformation. The work is motivated by the need for robust, real-time, patient-specific vessel identification during surgery.

Method: The method uses preoperative CT vessel annotations to synthesize ultrasound through optimized physics-based rendering, applies domain adaptation to intraoperative ultrasound, trains the rendering pipeline end-to-end for vessel identification and patient specificity, and introduces deformation-aware augmentation to simulate intraoperative motion and tissue deformation.

Result: In abdominal phantom experiments and a limited single-case clinical feasibility evaluation, the framework achieved real-time intrahepatic vessel-branch identification and maintained performance under new patient poses.

Conclusion: The proposed deformable ultrasound augmentation framework can enable real-time, patient-specific intrahepatic vessel identification without requiring preoperative ultrasound, and it remains usable under deformation and pose changes; however, broader multi-patient validation is still required.

Abstract: Purpose: Laparoscopic ultrasound (LUS) enhances the safety of liver surgery by visualizing intrahepatic vessels in real-time. Still, vessel identification remains difficult due to probe constraints, complex vascular structure, and tissue deformation. This work aims to enable real-time, patient-specific vessel identification that remains robust under deformation through deformable ultrasound augmentation. Methods: Preoperative CT vessel annotations are used to generate synthetic ultrasound data via optimized physics-based rendering, coupled with domain adaptation to intraoperative ultrasound. The rendering is trained end-to-end for vessel identification and patient-specificity, eliminating the need for preoperative ultrasound. A deformation-aware augmentation simulates realistic intraoperative motion and tissue deformation within the rendering pipeline. Results: In abdominal phantom and limited clinical feasibility experiments (single-case clinical evaluation), the framework achieved real-time intrahepatic vessel-branch identification, maintaining performance under new patient poses. Conclusion: The framework enables real-time vessel identification without preoperative ultrasound and supports technical feasibility, but multi-patient validation is still needed for generalizability and clinical feasibility.

</details>


### [13] [Optimizing Latent Representations for Robust Building Damage Assessment Onboard Earth Observation Satellites](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29575)
*Thomas Goudemant,Benjamin Francesconi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 作者提出一种星上AI损毁评估框架：把灾前影像压缩成潜在表示上传卫星，与灾后影像在星上比对，实现建筑级定位和损毁分类，从而减少下传数据量并缩短响应时间；xBD实验验证了其在错位和强压缩下的有效性。 / The paper proposes an onboard AI framework that uploads compressed latent features of pre-disaster imagery to satellites and compares them with post-disaster images onboard to localize buildings and classify damage, reducing downlink needs and latency; xBD experiments validate effectiveness under misalignment and strong compression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机是灾害或冲突后快速识别受损建筑对紧急响应非常关键，但传统流程受限于数据下传、地面处理和人工解译延迟，难以及时提供可行动信息，因此需要把损毁评估尽可能前移到数据源端。 / The motivation is that rapid damaged-building identification is critical for emergency response, but conventional pipelines suffer from latency due to data downlink, ground processing, and human interpretation, motivating damage assessment directly at the data source onboard satellites.

Method: 方法上，作者将灾前影像在地面编码为紧凑潜在表示并上传至卫星，再与星上新获取的灾后影像进行比较；系统设计围绕适配星上计算的模型变体展开，系统性评估了孪生处理、交叉注意力、潜在空间压缩和面向鲁棒性的数据增强。 / Methodologically, pre-disaster imagery is encoded on the ground into compact latent representations, transmitted to the satellite, and compared onboard with newly acquired post-event images. The study benchmarks onboard-compatible variants involving siamese processing, cross-attention, latent-space compression, and robustness-oriented data augmentation.

Result: 在xBD数据集上的实验表明，该系统能够可靠地进行建筑损毁评估；即使在存在影像错位的情况下仍具备稳健性，并且在强压缩下仅有很小的性能损失。 / Experiments on the xBD dataset show reliable building damage assessment, robustness under image misalignment, and minimal performance loss even with strong compression.

Conclusion: 该工作表明，基于灾前/灾后高分辨率光学影像的星上AI系统，可以在卫星端直接完成建筑物级损毁定位与分类，在较强压缩条件下仍保持较小性能下降，并对配准误差具有稳健性。 / The paper concludes that an onboard AI system can perform object-level building damage localization and classification directly on satellites, remaining robust to image misalignment and showing only limited degradation under strong latent compression.

Abstract: Rapid identification of damaged buildings after natural disasters or on war areas is crucial to support emergency response and prioritize interventions. Earth Observation constellations provide timely, large-scale coverage, but actionable information is often delayed by data downlink constraints, on-ground processing, and human interpretation. Reducing this latency is essential to improve decision-making responsiveness. In this work, we propose an original AI-based system that enables object-level building damage assessment (localization and damage classification) directly onboard satellites from pre-disaster and post-disaster highresolution optical imagery. Available pre-disaster images are encoded on ground into compact latent representations, transmitted to the satellite, and compared on-board with newly acquired post-event observations. Leveraging AI interpretation capabilities and increasing processing capabilities on-board satellites, the proposed design enables processing directly at the data source, reducing the amount of information to be downlinked while preserving task-relevant content and improving overall system responsivity. We explore the design space through a systematic benchmark of onboard-compatible variants, analyzing the impact of siamese processing, cross-attention, latent-space compression, and robustness-oriented data augmentation. Experiments on xBD dataset demonstrate reliable and robust damage assessment under misalignment, with minimal performance degradation under strong compression.

</details>


### [14] [ReactBench: A Cause-Driven Benchmark for Multimodal Hallucination via Systematic Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29579)
*Shizhe Zhou,Bohan Jia,Kai Wu,Yan Shen,Tongyun Li,Yuyang Wu,Shaohui Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ReactBench tests why MLLMs hallucinate, not just whether they hallucinate, and finds that current models still fail under targeted multimodal reasoning and perception challenges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing benchmarks mainly measure hallucination outcomes rather than causes, and often use overly simple settings that no longer sufficiently challenge state-of-the-art multimodal models.

Method: The paper builds a cause-driven benchmark using adversarial images, hallucination-inducing queries, multiple targeted tasks, exam-style evaluation, and Chain-of-Thought-based sub-cause analysis.

Result: Extensive experiments show that current MLLMs are still notably susceptible to cause-specific hallucination patterns, including biases and perceptual weaknesses exposed by the four designed tasks.

Conclusion: ReactBench is a systematic, interpretable benchmark for diagnosing multimodal hallucinations in MLLMs, showing that even strong current models remain vulnerable to specific causal triggers.

Abstract: While multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved rapid progress in vision-language understanding, they remain prone to multimodal hallucinations, producing responses that are inconsistent with the visual input. Existing benchmarks predominantly focus on detecting hallucination outcomes rather than evaluating the underlying causes of these failures. Moreover, many benchmarks rely on simplistic scenarios and limited evaluation formats that no longer challenge state-of-the-art models. To address these limitations, we introduce ReactBench, a cause-driven hallucination benchmark featuring multiple tasks and an exam-style evaluation format. By generating adversarial images and hallucination-inducing queries, ReactBench introduces four targeted tasks: Relational Erasure, Counterfactual Attribute, Alteration Tracing, and Dense Counting. These tasks systematically expose co-occurrence bias, language priors, cross-image comparative perception deficiencies, and fine-grained perceptual bottlenecks. Beyond standard accuracy-based evaluation, we leverage Chain-of-Thought reasoning to identify fine-grained sub-causes of hallucination within each task. Extensive evaluations reveal that current MLLMs remain notably vulnerable to cause-specific hallucination triggers, demonstrating the value of ReactBench as a systematic and interpretable testbed for diagnosing and improving multimodal model robustness. The project page is available at https://reactbench.github.io/.

</details>


### [15] [BitC-3DGS: High-Capacity 3D Gaussian Splatting Watermarking via Bit Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29583)
*Yuquan Bi,Baosheng Yu,Yingke Lei,Jianwei Yang,Hongsong Wang,Jie Gui,Yuan Yan Tang,James Tin-Yau Kwok*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BitC-3DGS compresses multiple watermark bits into each token so 3D Gaussian Splatting can carry much longer messages than CLIP-limited prior methods, reaching 128-bit capacity without major loss in decoding accuracy or visual quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3DGS watermarking methods based on pretrained text encoders are constrained to 77-bit messages because of CLIP’s fixed 77-token context length, which prevents embedding richer ownership, provenance, and authentication information needed in large-scale 3D asset pipelines.

Method: The paper proposes BitC-3DGS, a bit-compression framework that maps multiple message bits into a single semantic token through bit-compressed tokenization. It also introduces a dual-branch decoder for joint chunk decompression and bit decoding, plus a hard-message sampling strategy to improve decoder training coverage.

Result: Experiments on Blender and LLFF show that BitC-3DGS achieves high-capacity watermarking with high message recovery accuracy and good rendering fidelity. In particular, it supports 128-bit messages with recovery accuracy comparable to recent state-of-the-art 64-bit methods.

Conclusion: BitC-3DGS overcomes the 77-token limitation of CLIP-based bit-to-token watermarking and enables higher-capacity watermark embedding for 3D Gaussian Splatting while maintaining strong recovery accuracy and rendering quality.

Abstract: High-capacity watermarking is necessary for 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) assets to embed rich information (e.g., ownership, provenance, and authentication codes), enabling reliable identification and integrity verification in large-scale 3D asset pipelines. Existing bit-to-token watermarking methods based on a pre-trained text encoder are limited to 77-bit messages due to CLIP's fixed 77-token context length, as tokens beyond this limit are unsupported by learned positional embeddings. To address this limitation, we introduce BitC-3DGS, a bit-compression framework that encodes multiple message bits per token. It employs a bit-compressed tokenization scheme that encodes multiple bits within the same chunk into a single semantic token. To enable recovery of the compressed information, it further introduces a dual-branch architecture for joint chunk decompression and bit decoding, along with a hard-message sampling strategy to improve combinatorial coverage during decoder training. Extensive experiments on the Blender and LLFF datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of BitC-3DGS for high-capacity watermarking, achieving high message recovery accuracy and rendering fidelity. For example, it supports 128-bit message capacity with recovery accuracy comparable to that of 64-bit messages in recent state-of-the-art methods.

</details>


### [16] [Non-Forgetting Knowledge Allocation with Bi-level Competition for Class-Incremental Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29592)
*Xiang Tan,Run He,Yawen Cui,Mengchen Zhao,Yan Wu,Tianyi Chen,Huiping Zhuang,Xiaonan Luo,Guanbin Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: NoFA-BC solves two problems in adapter-based class-incremental learning for pre-trained models: uniform adapter usage and allocator forgetting. It builds a non-forgetting allocator with recursive least squares, then uses a two-stage competition mechanism to pick the most relevant adapter signals and suppress irrelevant ones, improving adaptive knowledge allocation and retention of old tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing adapter-based CIL methods use task-specific adapters but allocate knowledge uniformly during inference, ignoring task discrepancy and causing inefficient adapter usage. In addition, the allocator itself tends to forget under incremental task updates. The work is motivated by the need for adaptive, non-forgetting allocation of adapter knowledge.

Method: The method introduces NoFA-BC, which has three main components: a non-forgetting allocator trained via a recursive least-squares formulation to approximate training with all historical data; a bi-level competition mechanism consisting of intra-task Winner-Takes-All and inter-task Last-Ones-Fall to adaptively allocate adapter contributions; and a stability enhancement process to improve old-task performance.

Result: According to the abstract, the proposed approach enables per-input tailored adapter participation, suppresses irrelevant adapters, preserves allocator knowledge across evolving tasks, and further improves performance on old tasks. The abstract indicates better utilization of adapters and stronger old-task stability, though it does not provide numerical metrics.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that NoFA-BC improves class-incremental learning with pre-trained models by preventing allocator forgetting and by dynamically selecting and suppressing adapters according to input/task relevance, while a stability enhancement step further strengthens retention on old tasks.

Abstract: Class-Incremental Learning (CIL) with pre-trained models (PTMs) aims to sequentially adapt PTMs to new categories without forgetting old knowledge. Built upon PTMs, existing adapter-based methods mainly train models via distinct task-specific adapters, and present a uniform knowledge allocation for each adapter during inference. However, this allocation mechanism ignores the nature of task discrepancy and leads to suboptimal utilization of adapters. Also, under CIL constraint, an allocator is prone to forgetting when tasks evolve. To address these issues, we propose a Non-Forgetting Allocation with Bi-Level Competition (NoFA-BC). NoFA-BC constructs a non-forgetting allocator (NFA) by transforming the allocator training into a recursive least-squares problem and achieves an allocator equivalent to that trained with all data. Based on the NFA, a Bi-Level Competition (BLC) including an intra-task level Winner-Takes-All (WTA) mechanism and inter-task Last-Ones-Fall (LOF) elimination is proposed to provide better allocation of adapter knowledge. WTA extracts the most significant logit within a task to represent the adapter's contribution and LOF suppresses the irrelevant adapters. With BLC, participation ratio of each adapter can be tailored for each input. Moreover, a Stability Enhancement (SE) process is incorporated to further improve the performance of old tasks.

</details>


### [17] [CogniVerse: Revolutionizing Multi-Modal Retrieval-Augmented Generation with Cognitive Reflection and Geometric Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29602)
*Xiang Fang,Wanlong Fang,Changshuo Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: CogniVerse improves multimodal RAG by adaptively deciding when and what to retrieve, aligning multimodal knowledge more precisely, and generating answers that are both locally accurate and globally coherent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MMRAG systems for knowledge-intensive multimodal QA are limited by noisy or irrelevant retrieval, semantic misalignment across modalities, weak adaptive reasoning, and incoherent generation between local details and global context.

Method: The paper proposes CogniVerse with three components: a Cognitive Reflection Module to decide whether retrieval is needed and filter noisy multimodal evidence; a Multi-modal Retrieval Module that aligns embeddings in a Riemannian manifold and refines knowledge graphs using spectral graph theory; and a Hierarchical Generation Module trained with an optimal-transport-based loss to jointly optimize token-level correctness and global coherence.

Result: According to the abstract, extensive experiments show that CogniVerse significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in accuracy and coherence, while also reducing retrieval latency.

Conclusion: CogniVerse is an effective and efficient MMRAG framework that improves both answer accuracy and semantic coherence while also lowering retrieval latency through a cognitively inspired multi-stage design.

Abstract: Multi-modal Retrieval-Augmented Generation (MMRAG) has emerged as a powerful paradigm for enhancing Multimodal Large Language Models in knowledge-intensive question answering by integrating external visual, textual, and structural knowledge. However, existing MMRAG frameworks suffer from critical limitations, including noisy and irrelevant retrieval, cross-modal semantic misalignment, lack of adaptive reasoning, and incoherent generation across local and global contexts. We introduce \textbf{CogniVerse}, a novel MMRAG framework that addresses these challenges through a cognitive-inspired, mathematically rigorous approach. Drawing from human-like reasoning, CogniVerse integrates three synergistic components: (1) a Cognitive Reflection Module that dynamically assesses retrieval necessity and filters relevant multi-modal content, reducing noise and computational overhead; (2) a Multi-modal Retrieval Module that aligns embeddings in a Riemannian manifold using information geometry and refines knowledge graphs via spectral graph theory, ensuring precise and coherent retrieval; and (3) a Hierarchical Generation Module that employs an optimal transport-based loss to balance token-level accuracy and global semantic coherence. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CogniVerse significantly outperforms state-of-the-art systems in both accuracy and coherence, while reducing retrieval latency.

</details>


### [18] [AgentCVR: Active Multi-Agent Cross-Video Reasoning via Script-Simulated Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29643)
*Yilun Qiu,Jiahe Wang,Cilin Yan,Jiayin Cai,Xiaolong Jiang,Yao Hu,Chun Yuan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AgentCVR turns cross-video reasoning into an iterative multi-agent evidence search problem and uses a cheap RL training simulator, leading to stronger CVR performance than standard single-pass MLLM methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multimodal large language models handle multiple videos with simple single-pass compression into a shared context, which can hide sparse but crucial evidence. The paper is motivated by the need for a more active and targeted approach to evidence acquisition in cross-video reasoning.

Method: The paper proposes AgentCVR, a multi-agent framework with a Master Agent coordinating specialized Visual and Audio Agents to iteratively retrieve, align, and aggregate evidence across multiple videos. It also introduces Script-Simulated RL, which trains the policy using LLM-generated semantic scripts and a lightweight text simulator instead of expensive multimodal online exploration.

Result: On a comprehensive CVR benchmark, AgentCVR outperforms single-pass baselines and achieves performance comparable to state-of-the-art closed-source systems, with particular gains on complex cross-video alignment and localization tasks.

Conclusion: AgentCVR effectively improves cross-video reasoning by using a multi-agent, iterative evidence-acquisition process, and it reaches performance competitive with strong closed-source systems on difficult CVR tasks.

Abstract: Cross-Video Reasoning (CVR) has emerged as a critical frontier in multimodal intelligence, requiring models to retrieve, align, and aggregate evidence distributed across multiple videos. Current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) often struggle with CVR, as simple single-pass strategies encode multiple videos into a shared compressed context, potentially obscuring rare but critical evidence. In this paper, we propose AgentCVR, a multi-agent framework that treats CVR as an active evidence-acquisition task. AgentCVR employs a Master Agent to iteratively coordinate specialized Visual and Audio Agents for targeted evidence extraction. To ensure efficient training, we introduce Script-Simulated RL, which optimizes the agent's policy with LLM-generated semantic scripts and a lightweight text-based simulator, bypassing costly multimodal inference during online exploration. Experimental results on a comprehensive CVR benchmark show that AgentCVR outperforms single-pass baselines and achieves comparable performance to state-of-the-art closed-source systems, particularly in complex cross-video alignment and localization. To ensure reproducibility, our code is available at https://github.com/wang-jh24/AgentCVR.

</details>


### [19] [MARTIAN: A Rendering Framework for Aerial Mars Imagery from HiRISE Orbital Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29647)
*Dario Pisanti,Georgios Georgakis*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MARTIAN uses real Mars orbital maps plus Blender to create realistic, labeled synthetic aerial images for training Mars navigation systems, and the synthetic data transferred successfully to real Mars imagery.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vision-based aerial navigation on Mars must handle highly variable lighting and terrain, but progress is limited by the lack of large-scale annotated aerial datasets for training and evaluation.

Method: The paper introduces MARTIAN, a Blender-based rendering pipeline that uses real HiRISE orbital map products to synthesize aerial imagery of Martian terrain under controllable illumination and varying altitudes, while automatically providing precise pose annotations.

Result: The framework can generate realistic annotated Mars aerial observations and has been validated in concurrent work, where deep image matchers trained synthetically were successfully evaluated on real Mars imagery for map-based localization.

Conclusion: MARTIAN is an effective open-source synthetic data generation framework for Mars aerial navigation, producing realistic rendered views with accurate pose labels and demonstrating practical value by supporting real-image evaluation in Mars localization research.

Abstract: Aerial navigation on Mars requires vision-based pipelines that are robust to the diverse illumination conditions and terrain morphology of the Martian surface. A key bottleneck for training and evaluating such methods is the scarcity of large-scale, annotated aerial datasets. We present MARTIAN, an open-source Blender-based rendering framework that leverages real HiRISE orbital map products to synthesize realistic aerial views of the Martian terrain under controllable lighting conditions and at varying altitudes. MARTIAN generates observations with accurate pose annotations, directly addressing the scarcity of training data for vision-based navigation on Mars. The framework has been validated through its deployment in concurrent work on map-based localization systems for Ingenuity and future Mars rotorcraft, where synthetically trained deep image matchers were successfully evaluated on real Mars imagery. MARTIAN is publicly available at: https://github.com/nasa-jpl/martian.

</details>


### [20] [OccamToken: Efficient VLM Inference with Training-Free and Budget-Adaptive Token Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29657)
*Geng Li,Guohao Chen,Ting Chen,Shilin Shan,Kuangji Zuo,Bofan Lyu,Tuo An,Gen Li,Jianfei Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OccamToken prunes visual tokens by comparing them against register-token attention instead of using fixed top-K ranking, achieving strong accuracy with extreme token reduction and no extra training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual token pruning methods rely on absolute importance ranking and fixed token budgets, but this is brittle because attention sinks can distort rankings, image redundancy varies across inputs, and relevant visual evidence depends on the query.

Method: The method uses register tokens as a reference to judge whether each visual token contributes information beyond low-information attention patterns. It applies dynamic thresholds based on register attention to perform both image-adaptive redundancy pruning and query-adaptive relevance pruning, without additional training.

Result: Across LLaVA-NeXT, LLaVA-v1.5, and Qwen3-VL, OccamToken consistently improves the accuracy-efficiency trade-off. On LLaVA-NeXT, it compresses 2,880 visual tokens to about 40 while preserving more than 93% of original accuracy, showing stability even at 1.4% token retention.

Conclusion: OccamToken is a training-free visual token pruning framework that replaces brittle absolute top-K ranking with register-anchored relative evidence testing, enabling much more stable and aggressive token compression in VLMs while maintaining accuracy.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) rely on long visual token sequences for visual understanding, making the prefill stage expensive in both computation and memory. Most existing pruning methods follow an absolute-ranking paradigm, assigning importance scores to visual tokens and retaining a fixed top-K subset. In this work, we argue that this paradigm is fundamentally brittle: attention sinks distort token importance rankings, while image redundancy and query-dependent visual evidence make fixed token budgets unreliable across inputs. We propose OccamToken, a training-free framework that replaces absolute token ranking with register-anchored relative evidence testing. Instead of asking which tokens are globally important, OccamToken evaluates whether a visual token provides information beyond a register-based reference. Our key insight is that register tokens naturally absorb low-information attention patterns, making them a stable reference for identifying genuinely informative visual evidence. Based on this principle, OccamToken performs both image-adaptive redundancy pruning and query-adaptive relevance pruning through dynamic thresholds derived from register attention. Across LLaVA-NeXT, LLaVA-v1.5, and Qwen3-VL, OccamToken consistently improves the accuracy-efficiency trade-off without additional training. Notably, on LLaVA-NeXT, it reduces 2,880 visual tokens to approximately 40 while preserving over 93% of the original accuracy, enabling stable visual token compression even in the extreme 1.4% retention regime.

</details>


### [21] [VisualThink-VLA: Visual Intermediate Reasoning for Effective and Low-Latency Vision-Language-Action Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30011)
*Mingjian Gao,Wenqiao Zhang,Yuqian Yuan,Yang Dai,Binhe Yu,Zheqi Lv,Haoyu Zheng,Jiaqi Zhu,Zhiqi Ge,Zixuan Wan,Siliang Tang,Yueting Zhuang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 中文：这篇论文用“视觉证据”替代文本推理来驱动 VLA 动作决策，在保证性能的同时大幅降低延迟，更适合实时机器人控制。English: This paper replaces text-based reasoning with visual evidence to guide VLA action decisions, preserving performance while dramatically reducing latency for real-time robotic control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 中文：现有将显式推理引入 VLA 的工作多依赖文本 chain-of-thought，但在具身控制中，文本信息常与动作预测弱相关甚至产生干扰，而且自回归文本解码带来过高时延，不适合闭环实时执行。English: Prior reasoning-augmented VLA methods often rely on textual chain-of-thought, but in embodied control text can be weakly relevant or even interfere with action prediction, and autoregressive text decoding introduces excessive latency for real-time closed-loop execution.

Method: 中文：该方法使用紧凑的视觉证据接口来引导动作预测，保留空间信息并避免文本解码开销；同时采用定制的选择性路由机制学习视觉证据 token，以在保持专门化能力的同时实现低时延推理，并配套构建 VisualEvidence-Kit 进行监督与可审计评测。English: The method guides action prediction through a compact visual-evidence interface that preserves spatial precision while avoiding text decoding overhead; it further uses a tailored selective routing mechanism to learn visual-evidence tokens for low-latency yet specialized inference, and introduces VisualEvidence-Kit for supervision and audit.

Result: 中文：在多个基准和真实机器人评测中，VISUALTHINK-VLA 在大多数任务上取得最高成功率，并将推理增强基线的多秒级延迟降到亚秒级；例如在 BridgeData V2 上，单步延迟从 ECoT 的 8.377 秒降至 0.367 秒，达到 22.8 倍加速。English: Across multiple benchmarks and real-robot evaluations, VISUALTHINK-VLA achieves the highest success rate on most tasks while reducing multi-second latency of reasoning-augmented baselines to the sub-second regime; on BridgeData V2, step latency drops from 8.377s with ECoT to 0.367s, a 22.8x speedup.

Conclusion: 中文：VISUALTHINK-VLA 证明了视觉中间推理比文本链式推理更适合实时具身控制，能够同时提升成功率并显著降低推理延迟。English: VISUALTHINK-VLA shows that visual intermediate reasoning is better suited than textual chain-of-thought for real-time embodied control, improving success rates while drastically reducing inference latency.

Abstract: Recent work has begun to equip vision-language-action (VLA) policies with explicit intermediate reasoning. In embodied control, however, textual chain-of-thought is a poor fit: irrelevant or weakly textual information can interfere with action prediction, while autoregressive text decoding adds too much latency for real-time closed-loop execution. We present VISUALTHINK-VLA, a visual intermediate-reasoning framework for accurate, low-latency VLA policies. Our bootstrapping philosophy is to guide action with effective visual thinking: VISUALTHINK-VLA bootstraps action prediction through a compact visual-evidence interface that preserves spatial precision while avoiding decoding overhead. Besides, to further improve performance and efficiency, VISUALTHINK-VLA adopts a tailored selective routing mechanism to learn the visual evidence tokens, enabling low-latency inference while preserving high-capacity specialization. We also introduce VisualEvidence-Kit, a supervision-and-audit resource centered on a VisualEvidence-Agent that constructs a 754.7k VLA instructions VisualEvidence-Set for route supervision and counterfactual faithfulness tests. Across multiple benchmarks and real-robot evaluation, VISUALTHINK-VLA achieves the highest success rate on most benchmarks while reducing the multi-second latency of reasoning-augmented baselines to the sub-second regime. For example, on BridgeData V2, it reduces step latency from 8.377,s with ECoT to 0.367,s, achieving a 22.8 times speedup.

</details>


### [22] [FakeVLM-R1: Internalizing Physical Laws via CoT for Synthetic Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30062)
*Leqi Zhu,Junyan Ye,Kaiqing Lin,Zhiyuan Yan,Conghui He,Weijia Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FakeVLM-R1 improves AI-generated image detection by combining SFT, GRPO, and a critical-thinking CoT with physics-grounded evidence, achieving SOTA accuracy, better explanations, fewer false alarms on real images, and stronger robustness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing interpretable detection methods based on large multimodal models depend heavily on imitation learning from large forged-image corpora. As a result, they lack genuine causal reasoning and often produce explanatory hallucinations, limiting trustworthiness and real-image discrimination.

Method: The paper builds FakeVLM-R1 on top of supervised fine-tuning, then adds Group Relative Policy Optimization and a Critical Thinking Chain-of-Thought mechanism. At inference time, the model uses bidirectional dialectical reasoning: it proposes a forgery hypothesis while simultaneously constructing an authenticity counter-argument grounded in physical commonsense. The work also introduces the FakeClue++ dataset with annotations anchored in physical laws of real images.

Result: Across multiple benchmarks, FakeVLM-R1 achieves state-of-the-art performance among evaluated models. It delivers precise and interpretable detection, mitigates the over-rejection bias toward real images, and remains robust under perturbations.

Conclusion: FakeVLM-R1 can perform high-accuracy and logically interpretable synthetic image detection with stronger causal-style reasoning, reduced false rejection of real images, and better robustness/generalization than prior methods.

Abstract: The development of generative artificial intelligence technologies has propelled the visual realism of synthetic images to an unprecedented level. Although current interpretable detection methods based on Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) have made certain progress, they still rely on imitation learning derived from massive volumes of forged data. Consequently, they lack genuine causal reasoning capabilities and are prone to explanatory hallucinations. To overcome this bottleneck, we propose FakeVLM-R1, aiming to endow the model with human-like critical thinking capabilities when performing synthetic detection tasks. Building upon Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT), this framework integrates Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with a Critical Thinking Chain-of-Thought (CoT) mechanism. During the inference phase, the model executes a "bidirectional dialectical reasoning" process: while proposing a forgery hypothesis, it must simultaneously invoke physical commonsense to construct an authenticity counter-proof. Furthermore, we constructed the FakeClue++ dataset with high-quality samples, which extensively introduces annotations guided by the physical laws of authentic images, providing a unified authenticity anchor for the model. Experiments confirm that FakeVLM-R1 achieves SOTA performance the evaluated models across multiple benchmarks. It not only achieves high-precision, logically interpretable detection but also resolves the over-rejection bias of existing methods against real images, demonstrating generalization and robustness against perturbations.

</details>


### [23] [AnomalyAgent: Training-Free Agentic Models for Zero-/Few-Shot Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30140)
*Yi Zhang,Jiawen Zhu,Lele Fu,Guansong Pang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Benefiting from generalizability of vision-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP, many zero-/few-shot anomaly detection (AD) approaches have achieved impressive detection performance across various datasets. Nevertheless, they require substantial training on large auxiliary datasets to adapt VLMs to anomaly detection, and their inference largely relies on visual-text embedding similarity-based anomaly scores, lacking reasoning abilities to detect complex anomalies that require in-depth contextual understanding. To address this limitation, we propose \textbf{AnomalyAgent}, a novel training-free, agentic framework that leverages the advanced reasoning and generalization capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for anomaly detection. The key ingredients include \textbf{1)} a comprehensive anomaly-centric toolset that enables adaptive MLLM-driven, agentic anomaly reasoning in zero-shot settings, and \textbf{2)} a customized memory module that grounds anomaly reasoning with few-shot, in-context reference examples. We extend evaluation beyond the detection of simple anomalies (e.g., surface defects like cracks and dents and clear lesions) in widely used benchmarks to more diverse types of anomalies such as logical/contextual anomalies in logistics and manufacturing settings. Extensive experiment results demonstrate that our AnomalyAgent achieves substantially better performance compared to training-free VLM-based AD and generic agentic methods, highlighting its superior generalization capability in both zero-shot and few-shot anomaly detection settings. The code implementation can be find at this address.

### [24] [IP-Adapter Is All You Need: Towards Fine-Tuning-Free Diffusion-Based Talking Face Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30230)
*Hao Wu,Xiangyang Luo,Hao Wang,Jiawei Zhang,Yi Zhang,Jinwei Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free talking-face diffusion system reuses Stable Diffusion and IP-Adapter plus three parameter-free control modules to improve identity preservation, lip-sync, and temporal stability beyond prior methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing diffusion-based talking face generation methods depend on task-specific fine-tuning and large-scale audiovisual datasets, causing high computational cost and limiting scalability and accessibility.

Method: The method directly uses pretrained Stable Diffusion and IP-Adapter without fine-tuning, and introduces three training-free components: Structurist for disentangling and reassembling lip and appearance features, Structure Controller for adaptive embedding refinement based on quasi-monotonic motion trends, and Noise Sensor for Gaussian-prior-based suppression of flicker and jitter.

Result: Experiments show the proposed method outperforms prior state-of-the-art approaches, with at least 0.16 improvement in PCLD for lip-sync accuracy and at least 0.7 improvement in FID for visual fidelity.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that a fine-tuning-free talking face generation framework built on pretrained Stable Diffusion and IP-Adapter can surpass existing state-of-the-art methods in lip-sync accuracy and visual fidelity while avoiding task-specific training and large audiovisual datasets.

Abstract: With the rapid advancement of diffusion models, talking face generation has made remarkable progress. However, existing diffusion-based methods still require task-specific fine-tuning and large-scale audiovisual datasets, resulting in high computational costs that hinder scalability and accessibility of diffusion-based approaches across the research community. To address this, we propose a finetuning-free paradigm that directly performs talking face generation using the pretrained weights of Stable Diffusion and IP-Adapter. This backbone leverages the visual embedding capability of IP-Adapter to mine lip-related semantics from the pretrained Stable Diffusion. To address the challenges of identity drift, synchronization errors, and temporal instability, we also design three trainable-parameterfree components: (1) the Structurist, which explicitly disentangles and reassembles lip and appearance features to mitigate identity drift and appearance distortion; (2) the Structure Controller, which adaptively refines embeddings based on quasi-monotonic motion trends for precise lip synchronization; and (3) the Noise Sensor, which introduces Gaussian prior to detect and suppress flicker and jitter artifacts and enhance temporal consistency. Experimental results show that our method outperforms existing SOTA approaches in both lip-sync accuracy (at least 0.16 gain in PCLD) and visual fidelity (at least 0.7 improvement in FID), establishing a novel fine-tuning-free diffusion framework for talking face generation.

</details>


### [25] [Beyond 3D VQAs: Injecting 3D Spatial Priors into Vision-Language Models for Enhanced Geometric Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30231)
*Chun-Hsiao Yeh,Shengyi Qian,Manchen Wang,Yi Ma,Joseph Tighe,Fanyi Xiao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GASP improves VLM 3D spatial reasoning by teaching transformer layers geometric priors through correspondence and depth supervision, leading to much better internal geometry matching and strong benchmark gains without 3D VQA fine-tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLMs perform poorly on robust 3D spatial reasoning; 3D VQA fine-tuning can overfit dataset biases, and specialized 3D encoders are inflexible. The authors aim to build spatial understanding from fundamental geometric priors instead of only high-level QA supervision.

Method: The paper proposes GASP, which adds a small correspondence head with deep supervision across all transformer layers and trains it with a dual objective: contrastive learning on ground-truth point correspondences for 2D view invariance, and depth consistency supervision to reduce 3D geometric ambiguity, using large-scale video scene geometry.

Result: Standard VLMs have very low internal correspondence accuracy, often under 5%. GASP raises peak layer-wise correspondence to over 70%, keeps over 85% temporal robustness, and improves downstream benchmarks by +18.2% on All-Angles Bench and +29.0% on VSI-Bench, without using 3D VQA training data.

Conclusion: GASP shows that injecting geometric priors directly into LLM transformer layers is an effective and generalizable way to improve VLMs' 3D spatial reasoning without relying on 3D VQA fine-tuning.

Abstract: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) often struggle with robust 3D spatial reasoning. Prevailing methods that rely on fine-tuning with 3D visual question-answering (VQA) datasets may overfit dataset-specific biases, while integrating specialized 3D visual encoders is often inflexible and cumbersome. In this paper, we argue that genuine spatial understanding should emerge from learning fundamental geometric priors, not only from high-level VQA supervision. We propose GASP (Geometric-Aware Spatial Priors), a framework that injects these priors directly into the LLM's transformer layers. GASP employs a small correspondence head, applied as a deep supervision signal across all layers, and is trained with a dual objective leveraging ground-truth geometry from large-scale video scenes: a contrastive loss on ground-truth point correspondences enforces 2D view-invariance, while a depth consistency supervision resolves 3D geometric ambiguities. Our analysis first provides a diagnostic showing that standard VLMs' internal correspondence matching accuracy is very low (often below 5%). We then demonstrate that our training substantially improves this behavior, boosting peak layer-wise correspondence to over 70% and maintaining over 85% temporal robustness while baselines remain below 5%. These internal improvements translate to significant gains on downstream spatial benchmarks including +18.2% on All-Angles Bench and +29.0% on VSI-Bench, all without training on any 3D VQA data. Our findings indicate that learning from fundamental geometric priors is a promising and generalizable pathway towards VLMs with more reliable 3D spatial reasoning.

</details>


### [26] [BullingerDB: A Dataset for Handwritten Text Recognition and Writer Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30235)
*Marco Peer,Anna-Scius Bertrand,Patricia Scheurer,Andreas Fischer*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: BullingerDB is a large multilingual historical manuscript benchmark; TrOCR reaches 9.1% CER, and writer retrieval works reasonably well but remains challenging because handwriting style changes over time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing historical document benchmarks are limited in scale, linguistic diversity, temporal coverage, and writer variability; the authors aim to support more realistic evaluation for multilingual historical text recognition and time-aware writer analysis.

Method: The paper constructs a large-scale dataset from Heinrich Bullinger’s correspondence and evaluates baseline tasks including handwritten text recognition with models such as TrOCR and writer retrieval with a newly introduced temporal nDCG metric.

Result: The dataset contains 20,898 pages and 499,222 text lines from 796 writers over six decades. TrOCR achieves the best text recognition performance with 9.1% CER. Writer retrieval shows temporally coherent retrieval is possible, but mAP of 78.3% suggests difficulty from long-term style variation.

Conclusion: BullingerDB provides a strong new benchmark for multilingual historical document analysis, especially for text recognition and temporally-aware writer retrieval under long-term stylistic variation.

Abstract: We present BullingerDB, a large-scale benchmark dataset for historical document analysis based on the correspondence of Heinrich Bullinger (1504-1575). The corpus comprises 20,898 pages and 499,222 text lines written by 796 writers over six decades, featuring stylistic variation, multilingual content (mostly Latin and Early New High German) as well as meta-information such as writer identity and time. We evaluate BullingerDB on text recognition and writer retrieval. TrOCR, the best performing model, achieves a CER of 9.1%. For writer retrieval, we introduce a temporal nDCG metric to assess time-aware retrieval. While temporally coherent retrieval is achievable, mAP (78.3%) scores indicate challenges due to long-term stylistic variation. With BullingerDB, we aim to establish a new benchmark for multilingual historical text recognition and temporally-aware writer analysis.

</details>


### [27] [SAM3D-Phys: Towards Multi-Object Interactive Simulation in Real World](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30239)
*Xin Dong,Weijian Deng,Lihan Zhang,Tianru Dai,Wenfeng Deng,Yansong Tang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SAM3D-Phys combines multi-view scene reconstruction, SAM3D-based 3D completion, physics-constrained alignment, and mask-guided texture refinement to turn incomplete reconstructed objects into complete, scene-consistent assets for multi-object physical simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multi-view scene reconstruction methods produce visually plausible scenes but often leave objects incomplete because of occlusions and limited viewpoints, which makes the reconstructed objects unsuitable for downstream physics simulation and interaction.

Method: The method first reconstructs a scene from multi-view images to obtain scene geometry and partial object observations, then uses SAM3D as a generative 3D prior to complete the missing object geometry. It restores scene-consistent object states through a physics-constrained spatial optimization algorithm for pose/location alignment and a mask-guided appearance distillation module for texture refinement.

Result: The framework produces clean object representations with recovered geometry, restored pose, and refined appearance that are suitable for physics-based simulation in reconstructed real-world scenes, including simultaneous interaction among multiple objects.

Conclusion: SAM3D-Phys can recover complete, scene-consistent, and physically simulatable object geometry from partial real-scene reconstructions, enabling simultaneous physically consistent interaction with multiple objects in reconstructed environments.

Abstract: This work addresses the problem of recovering complete, simulatable object geometry from reconstructed real-world scenes, enabling physics-based interaction with objects embedded in the scene. While modern multi-view reconstruction methods can produce visually accurate environments, objects are often incomplete due to occlusions and limited observations, making them unsuitable for physics simulation.
